Remark 7.26
.

In relation to Remark
7.23
, we note that the fourth term on the right hand side of (
225
) identically vanishes upon projection. All the terms in the third and fourth lines on the right hand side of (
227
) can be unpacked and seen not to depend on derivatives of
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}(\epsilon)
, whereas the first term on the last line identically vanishes upon projection.

Proposition 7.27
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
such that
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(0)\equiv 0
, we have

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{{\boldsymbol{4}}}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}))=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}^{\circ}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma})+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

(229)

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{3}}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}))=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}^{\circ}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma})+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

(230)

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}))=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\circ}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma})+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

(231)

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}))=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,.

(232)

Proof.

This is immediate from the formulae of Proposition
7.22
.

∎

We will not need formulae analogous to those of Proposition
7.22
for general higher rank tensors. It suffices to have the following propositions.

Proposition 7.28
.

For any
X\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{M}^{*})
, we have

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}}))

\displaystyle=\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{X}^{\circ}\,\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

(233)

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\chibh^{\circ}}))

\displaystyle=\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{X}^{\circ}\,\boldsymbol{\chibh^{\circ}})+\textup{nonlinear terms}

(234)

and

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}}))

\displaystyle=\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

(235)

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\chibh^{\circ}}))

\displaystyle=\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\chibh^{\circ}})+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,.

(236)

The same identities hold for
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
and
\boldsymbol{\alphab}
replacing
\boldsymbol{\chih}
and
\boldsymbol{\chibh}
.

Proof.

We start by noting that we have

\Pi\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}}=\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}}+\text{nonlinear terms}\,.

We compute

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}}))

\displaystyle=\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})+\text{nonlinear terms}

\displaystyle=\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{X}^{\circ}\,\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

where the second equality is easy to check and follows from the schematic formula

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{X}^{\circ}\,\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})

\displaystyle+(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}\,\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})(\ef,\boldsymbol{e_{I}})\cdot\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{3}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{I}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}\,\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})(\et,\boldsymbol{e_{I}})\cdot\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{4}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{I}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\,,

for which the last two terms are nonlinear. We also compute

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}}))

\displaystyle=\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})+\text{nonlinear terms}

\displaystyle=\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

where the second equality is easy to check and follows from the schematic formula

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})

\displaystyle+(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})(\boldsymbol{e_{I}},\boldsymbol{e_{J}})\cdot\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{3}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{I}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{J}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})(\boldsymbol{e_{I}},\boldsymbol{e_{J}})\cdot\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{4}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{I}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{J}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{I}}\,\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})(\ef,\boldsymbol{e_{J}})\cdot\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{I}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{3}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{J}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{I}}\,\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})(\et,\boldsymbol{e_{J}})\cdot\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{I}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{4}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{J}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\,,

for which the last four terms are nonlinear. Analogous considerations apply to
\boldsymbol{\chibh^{\circ}}
.

∎

Remark 7.29
.

It is manifest from the proof of Proposition
7.28
that
none of the
nonlinear terms
in the formulae
(
233
)
and
(
234
)
contain any
derivatives
of
\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}}(\epsilon)
,
(\Pi\boldsymbol{\chih^{\circ}})(\epsilon)
or
\boldsymbol{\chibh^{\circ}}(\epsilon)
,
(\Pi\boldsymbol{\chibh^{\circ}})(\epsilon)
respectively (or any derivatives of connection coefficients or curvature components)
.

We now state the relevant formulae for the Lie derivatives. Proposition
7.30
states the formula for the Lie derivative of one-tensors. Proposition
7.31
states the formula for the Lie derivative of the metric, together with a useful formula for the covariant derivative of the metric.

Proposition 7.30
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
and any
X\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{M}^{*})
, we have

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}))=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}^{\circ}_{X}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}([X,\et]))\Pi(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}([X,\ef]))\Pi(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

(237)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}(e_{3}^{\text{as}})\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(e_{4}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat})\,.

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})=

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}(e_{3}^{\text{as}})(e_{4}^{\text{as}})_{\flat})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}^{\circ}_{X}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}-\frac{1}{2}((\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})(\et))(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}-\frac{1}{2}((\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})(\ef))(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}+\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}(e_{3}^{\text{as}})(e_{4}^{\text{as}})_{\flat})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}^{\circ}_{X}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}([X,\et]))(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}([X,\ef]))(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,X(\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}(e_{3}^{\text{as}}))(e_{4}^{\text{as}})_{\flat}+\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}(e_{3}^{\text{as}})\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}((e_{4}^{\text{as}})_{\flat})\,.

∎

The following proposition states two important identities.

Proposition 7.31
.

For any
X\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{M}^{*})
, we have

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}(\Pi\boldsymbol{g}))=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\,\not{g}_{a,M}^{AB}\,\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\textup{as}}_{3},e^{\textup{as}}_{A})\,\left(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}(e_{4}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat})\otimes(e_{B}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat}+(e_{B}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}(e_{4}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat})\right)

(238)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,\not{g}_{a,M}^{AB}\,\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\textup{as}}_{4},e^{\textup{as}}_{A})\,\left(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}(e_{3}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat})\otimes(e_{B}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat}+(e_{B}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}(e_{3}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat})\right)

\displaystyle+\textup{nonlinear terms}

and

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(\Pi\boldsymbol{g}))=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}^{\circ}_{X}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})+\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ}([X,\et],\ea)\left(\Pi(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}+\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\right)

(239)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ}([X,\ef],\ea)\left(\Pi(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}+\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,\not{g}_{a,M}^{AB}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ}(e^{\textup{as}}_{3},e^{\textup{as}}_{A})\left(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(e_{4}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat})\otimes(e_{B}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat}+(e_{B}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(e_{4}^{\textup{as}})_{\flat})\right)

\displaystyle+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,.

Proof.

To prove the formula (
238
), one simply applies the Definition
7.9
and the compatibility identity
\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{X}\boldsymbol{g}=0
. To prove the formula (
239
), we first observe that

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(\Pi\boldsymbol{g}))=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ}))+\text{nonlinear terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})+\frac{1}{2}\,\not{g}_{a,M}^{AB}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ}(e^{\text{as}}_{3},e^{\text{as}}_{A})\left(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(e_{4}^{\text{as}})_{\flat})\otimes(e_{B}^{\text{as}})_{\flat}+(e_{B}^{\text{as}})_{\flat}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}(e_{4}^{\text{as}})_{\flat})\right)

\displaystyle+\text{nonlinear terms}\,,

where the first equality follows from Proposition
7.11
, the second equality from Proposition
7.7
. We also have

\displaystyle\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}^{\circ}_{X}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})-\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})(\et,\ea)\cdot\Pi(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})(\ea,\et)\cdot\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})(\ea,\ef)\cdot\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{X}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})(\ef,\ea)\cdot\Pi(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+\text{nonlinear terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}^{\circ}_{X}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})+\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ}([X,\et],\ea)\cdot\Pi(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ}([X,\et],\ea)\cdot\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ}([X,\ef],\ea)\cdot\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ}([X,\ef],\ea)\cdot\Pi(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\eb)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+\text{nonlinear terms}\,.

Combining the two computations yields the formula (
239
).

∎

Remark 7.32
.

The reader should note that the identity (
238
) implies the incompatibility relation

\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{e_{A}^{\textup{as}}}(\Pi\boldsymbol{g}))\neq 0

when
|\epsilon|>0
. The incompatibility arises because our geometric projection is performed relative to a frame (i.e.
\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}
) that is
not
null relative to
\boldsymbol{g}(\epsilon)
when
|\epsilon|>0
.

We conclude the section by noting that the projection formulae of Section
7.3.2
and the formulae derived in the present section can be combined together to treat the projection of all the differential operators defined in Section
4
. For instance, using Propositions
7.11
and
7.18
, one can state and prove the following proposition.

Proposition 7.33
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle-2\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}))+\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}))^{\mathsf{T}}-(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1})^{\circ})^{AB}(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})))_{AB}\,(\Pi\,\boldsymbol{g})

(240)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}-{\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\Pi(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\Pi\boldsymbol{g}}\,\Pi(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}-{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\Pi(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\Pi\boldsymbol{g}}\,\Pi(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

\displaystyle+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,.

Proof.

We compute

-2\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}}{}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})=\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})+\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})^{\mathsf{T}}-(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1})^{\circ})^{AB}(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}))_{AB}\,(\Pi\,\boldsymbol{g})+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,.

Using then the formula (
223
), we get

\displaystyle-2\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}}{}^{\circ}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})=

\displaystyle\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}))-\frac{1}{2}\left({\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{\boldsymbol{B}}\right)\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}-\frac{1}{2}\left({\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{\boldsymbol{B}}\right)\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

(241)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\Pi(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\Pi(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

(242)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\,g_{a,M}^{-1}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ},\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}^{\circ})\cdot{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\otimes\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{B}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}

(243)

\displaystyle+(\text{line}\,\eqref{ex_eq_line_1}+\text{line}\,\eqref{ex_eq_line_2}+\text{line}\,\eqref{ex_eq_line_3})^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle-(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1})^{\circ})^{AB}(\text{line}\,\eqref{ex_eq_line_1}+\text{line}\,\eqref{ex_eq_line_2}+\text{line}\,\eqref{ex_eq_line_3})_{AB}(\Pi\boldsymbol{g})

\displaystyle+\text{nonlinear terms}\,.

Note that in the formula (
240
) we dropped the nonlinear term

\frac{1}{2}\,g_{a,M}^{-1}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ},\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}^{\circ})\cdot{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\Pi\boldsymbol{g}}\,\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{B}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}\,.

∎

Remark 7.34
.

The
nonlinear terms
in the formula (
240
) contain derivatives of
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
and
(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})(\epsilon)
. These terms originate from replacing
(\Pi\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})(\epsilon)
by
(\Pi\boldsymbol{g})(\epsilon)
in the formula.

7.4

Restriction

We start by defining the restriction of
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
-horizontal
k
-tensors to
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}

k
-tensors.

Definition 7.35
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}

k
-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
, we define the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}

k
-tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
such that

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(e_{A_{1}}^{\textup{as}},\ldots,e_{A_{k}}^{\textup{as}})=(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})(e_{A_{1}}^{\textup{as}},\ldots,e_{A_{k}}^{\textup{as}})\,.

Remark 7.36
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}

k
-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
, we have

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(0)\equiv\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(0)\,.

With a slight abuse of notation (see later Remark
7.38
), we give the following definition.

Definition 7.37
.

We define the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
two-tensors
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)
and
\widetilde{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}(\epsilon)
such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(e_{A}^{\textup{as}},e_{B}^{\textup{as}})

\displaystyle=(\Pi\,\boldsymbol{g})(e_{A}^{\textup{as}},e_{B}^{\textup{as}})\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}(e_{A}^{\textup{as}},e_{B}^{\textup{as}})

\displaystyle=(\Pi\,\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})(e_{A}^{\textup{as}},e_{B}^{\textup{as}})\,.

Remark 7.38
.

We remark that
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)
is the restriction to
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
of
(\Pi\boldsymbol{g})(\epsilon)
, instead of
(\Pi\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\circ})(\epsilon)
. For a preliminary discussion of why this slight abuse of notation will be later convenient, see the overview in Section
7.1
.

Remark 7.39
.

In general, the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
two-tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}}}(\epsilon)
(defined according to Definition
7.35
) differs from both the standard volume form associated to
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)
and the standard volume form associated to
\widetilde{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}(\epsilon)
.

Definition 7.40
.

We define the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
contravariant tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}(\epsilon)
such that

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}(\not{g}_{a,M}(e_{A}^{\textup{as}},\cdot),\not{g}_{a,M}(e_{B}^{\textup{as}},\cdot))=(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1})^{\circ})(g_{a,M}(e_{A}^{\textup{as}},\cdot),g_{a,M}(e_{B}^{\textup{as}},\cdot))\,.

We adopt the notation

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}{}^{AB}=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}(\not{g}_{a,M}(e_{A}^{\text{as}},\cdot),\not{g}_{a,M}(e_{B}^{\text{as}},\cdot))\,.

In view of Propositions
7.11
and
7.13
, we have the following proposition.

Proposition 7.41
.

We have the identities

\widetilde{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}(\epsilon)=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)+\textup{nonlinear terms}

(244)

and

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}{}^{BC}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}_{AC}=\delta_{A}^{B}+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,.

(245)

Let the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
one-tensor

\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{I}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}(\epsilon)

be the restriction of
\Pi(\boldsymbol{e_{I}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}(\epsilon)
to
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
. We have the following definition for the restriction of covariant and Lie derivatives.

Definition 7.42
.

For any
\boldsymbol{f}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M}^{*})
, any
\boldsymbol{X}\in\Gamma(T\mathcal{M}^{*})
and any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}

k
-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}
, we define the smooth scalar function
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{X}}\,\boldsymbol{f}
such that

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{X}}\,\boldsymbol{f}=\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{X}}\,\boldsymbol{f})\,,

the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
one-tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\boldsymbol{f}
such that

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\boldsymbol{f})(e_{A}^{\textup{as}})

\displaystyle=(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}\boldsymbol{f}))(e_{A}^{\textup{as}})\,,

the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}

k
-tensors
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{X}}\widetilde{\varsigma}}
and
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\mathcal{L}}}_{\boldsymbol{X}}\widetilde{\varsigma}}
such that

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{X}}\widetilde{\varsigma}})(e_{A_{1}}^{\textup{as}},\ldots,e_{A_{k}}^{\textup{as}})

\displaystyle=(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla_{\boldsymbol{X}}}\,(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma^{\circ}})))(e_{A_{1}}^{\textup{as}},\ldots,e_{A_{k}}^{\textup{as}})\,,

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\mathcal{L}}}_{\boldsymbol{X}}\,\widetilde{\varsigma}})(e_{A_{1}}^{\textup{as}},\ldots,e_{A_{k}}^{\textup{as}})

\displaystyle=(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}_{\boldsymbol{X}}}\,(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma^{\circ}})))(e_{A_{1}}^{\textup{as}},\ldots,e_{A_{k}}^{\textup{as}})

and the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}

(k+1)
-tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}
such that

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(e_{A_{1}}^{\textup{as}},\ldots,e_{A_{k}}^{\textup{as}})

\displaystyle=(\Pi(\boldsymbol{\nabla}\,(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma^{\circ}})))(e_{A_{1}}^{\textup{as}},\ldots,e_{A_{k}}^{\textup{as}})\,.

Remark 7.43
.

The
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)
is a metric tensor on
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\epsilon)
is a linear connection over the bundle of
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
tensors. The latter does
not
coincide with the Levi-Civita connection of
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)

for all
|\epsilon|\geq 0
. In particular,

(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{e_{A}^{\textup{as}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}})(\epsilon)\not\equiv 0

(246)

when
|\epsilon|>0
(see already Remark
7.32
). Although the incompatibility relation (
246
) originates from our particular geometric procedure, the connection
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\epsilon)
also inherits the torsion of the original connection
\boldsymbol{\nablasl}(\epsilon)
on
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}

for all
|\epsilon|\geq 0
(see Remark
4.34
), which is a general aspect when one treats non-integrable structures.

Remark 7.44
.

We have the identity

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(0)\equiv\not{g}_{a,M}

between metric tensors on
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
and the identity

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(0)\equiv\nablasl_{a,M}

between linear connections over the bundle of
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
tensors.

We need one last definition in order to state our final formulae in Proposition
7.47
.

Definition 7.45
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensors
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}(\epsilon)
, we define the products

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}

\displaystyle:=(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}})^{AB}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}_{A}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{B}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}

\displaystyle:=(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}){}^{AC}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}){}^{BD}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}}}_{CD}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}_{A}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{B}

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}-(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}

and the differential operators

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\textbf{div}}}\,\widetilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle:=(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}})^{AB}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{AB}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\textbf{curl}}}\,\widetilde{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle:=(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}){}^{AC}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}){}^{BD}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}}}_{CD}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{AB}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}+(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})^{\mathsf{T}}-(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}})^{AB}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{AB}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}})\,.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
two-tensors
\boldsymbol{\theta}(\epsilon)
and
\boldsymbol{\omega}(\epsilon)
, we define

\displaystyle\textbf{tr}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}

\displaystyle:=(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}})^{AB}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{AB}\,,

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}})_{A}

\displaystyle:=(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}})^{BC}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{AC}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{B}\,,

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\omega}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}

\displaystyle:=(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}})^{AD}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}})^{CB}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{AC}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{BD}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\omega}}

\displaystyle:=(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}})^{AE}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}})^{DF}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}})^{CB}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}}}_{EF}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{AC}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_{BD}\,,

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\textbf{div}}}\,\widetilde{\theta}})_{A}

\displaystyle:=(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}})^{CB}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{C}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})_{AB}\,.

Remark 7.46
.

In view of the identity (
245
), the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
tensors
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}
are
traceless
relative to
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}
up to nonlinear terms.

The following proposition combines all the formulae derived in the previous sections and may be seen as the final product of our geometric procedure. The proof is immediate.

Proposition 7.47
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensors
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}(\epsilon)
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
two-tensor
\boldsymbol{\omega}(\epsilon)
, we have

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\otimes\boldsymbol{\theta}}

and the identities between smooth scalar functions

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\varsigma},\boldsymbol{\theta})_{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}\,,

\displaystyle\textbf{tr}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\omega}}

\displaystyle=\textbf{tr}_{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}\,\boldsymbol{\omega}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}\,\boldsymbol{\theta}\,.

In particular, we have

|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}|^{2}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}=|\boldsymbol{\varsigma}|^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}

(247)

and

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\boldsymbol{\theta}}+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
two-tensor
\boldsymbol{\theta}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{A}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{A}\,,

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}){}^{BC}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}}}_{AC}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}_{B}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{{}^{\boldsymbol{\star}}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{A}\,.

For any
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M}^{*})
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}f=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}f}-\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}f)\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}-\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}f)\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,.

(248)

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}+(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\etab}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}

(249)

\displaystyle+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}+(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}-(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{\not{g}_{a,M}}\cdot\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}

(250)

\displaystyle+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}+\frac{1}{2}\left({\boldsymbol{\chib}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}}{}_{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\boldsymbol{B}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{\boldsymbol{B}}\right)\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}+\frac{1}{2}\left({\boldsymbol{\chi}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}}{}_{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\boldsymbol{B}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{\boldsymbol{B}}\right)\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}

(251)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{\not{g}_{a,M}}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\chi}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}}{}_{\boldsymbol{A}}^{\boldsymbol{B}}\,\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{B}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}

\displaystyle+\textup{nonlinear terms}

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}+\frac{1}{2}\left({\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{\boldsymbol{B}}\right)\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\otimes\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}+\frac{1}{2}\left({\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{\boldsymbol{B}}\right)\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\otimes\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}

(252)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\otimes\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\otimes\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{\not{g}_{a,M}}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\,\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\otimes\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{B}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}

\displaystyle+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,.

We also have the formulae

\displaystyle\not{\textbf{div}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}=

Remark 7.48
.

We recall the Remarks
7.23
and
7.29
.
The
nonlinear terms
in the formulae
(
249
), (
250
), (
251
), (

), (

), (
257
), (
266
), (
267
)
and
(

do

derivatives
of the respective quantities (or any derivatives of connection coefficients or curvature components)
. On the other hand, some of the

in the formulae for the angular derivatives of tensors
do

). See the related Remark
7.34
.

7.4.1

Auxiliary identities

In this section, we collect some auxiliary propositions. These propositions will not be directly needed to formulate the renormalised system of vacuum Einstein equations (see Section
8
), but will later allow for convenient manipulations of some of the terms appearing in the system. Immediate proofs are omitted.

Proposition 7.49
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}

k
-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
, we have the identity

\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{\boldsymbol{A}_{1}\cdots\boldsymbol{A}_{k}}(\epsilon)=(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}_{1}})^{B_{1}}(\epsilon)\cdots(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}_{k}})^{B_{k}}(\epsilon)\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}_{B_{1}\cdots B_{k}}(\epsilon)+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,.

(270)

Proposition 7.50
.

We have the identities

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}}_{4}(\epsilon))_{\not{g}_{a,M}}\cdot\boldsymbol{g}(e_{4}^{\textup{as}},e_{3}^{\textup{as}})+(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon),\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{4}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}(\epsilon))_{\not{g}_{a,M}}

\displaystyle=\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

\displaystyle-2\,(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}}_{3}(\epsilon))_{\not{g}_{a,M}}+(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon),\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{3}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}(\epsilon))_{\not{g}_{a,M}}

\displaystyle=\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}_{4}(\epsilon)\cdot\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\textup{as}}_{4},e^{\textup{as}}_{3})+2

\displaystyle=\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}_{4}(\epsilon))^{2}\cdot\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\textup{as}}_{3},e^{\textup{as}}_{3})+2\,\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}_{4}(\epsilon)\cdot\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}_{3}(\epsilon)\cdot\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\textup{as}}_{4},e^{\textup{as}}_{3})

\displaystyle=\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}((\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})(\epsilon),(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{\boldsymbol{B}})(\epsilon))-\delta_{\boldsymbol{AB}}

\displaystyle=\textup{nonlinear terms}

and

\displaystyle(\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{3}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}})_{A}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}_{4}(\epsilon)\cdot\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\textup{as}}_{3},e_{A}^{\textup{as}})+(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}^{\sharp}(\epsilon)\cdot\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon))_{A}+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,.

All the inner products are products of
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
tensors relative to
\not{g}_{a,M}
and indices are raised by
\not{g}^{-1}_{a,M}
.

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{g}(\ef(\epsilon),\ea(\epsilon))

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\text{as}}_{4},(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)e^{\text{as}}_{3}+\not{g}_{a,M}^{CB}(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{C}(\epsilon)e^{\text{as}}_{B})+\text{nonlinear terms}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)\cdot\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\text{as}}_{4},e^{\text{as}}_{3})+\not{g}_{a,M}^{CB}(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{C}(\epsilon)\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\text{as}}_{4},e^{\text{as}}_{B})+\text{nonlinear terms}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}}_{4}(\epsilon))\cdot\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\text{as}}_{4},e^{\text{as}}_{3})+\not{g}_{a,M}^{CB}(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{C}(\epsilon)\boldsymbol{g}(\ef(\epsilon),e^{\text{as}}_{B})+\text{nonlinear terms}\,,

where in the last identity we used the identity (
270
). We compute

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{g}(\et(\epsilon),\ea(\epsilon))

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{g}(\et(\epsilon),(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)e^{\text{as}}_{3}+(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{3})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)e^{\text{as}}_{4}+\not{g}_{a,M}^{CB}(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{C}(\epsilon)e^{\text{as}}_{B})

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{3})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)\cdot\boldsymbol{g}(\et(\epsilon),e^{\text{as}}_{4})+\not{g}_{a,M}^{CB}(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{C}(\epsilon)\boldsymbol{g}(\et(\epsilon),e^{\text{as}}_{B})+\text{nonlinear terms}

\displaystyle=-2\,(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}}_{3}(\epsilon))+\not{g}_{a,M}^{CB}(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{C}(\epsilon)\boldsymbol{g}(\et(\epsilon),e^{\text{as}}_{B})+\text{nonlinear terms}\,,

where in the last identity we used the identity (
270
). We compute

\displaystyle-2

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{g}(\ef(\epsilon),\et(\epsilon))

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}((\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})(\epsilon),(\boldsymbol{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{\boldsymbol{B}})(\epsilon))+\text{nonlinear terms}\,.

∎

Proposition 7.53
.

\displaystyle[\ef(\epsilon),\et(\epsilon)]=

\displaystyle+(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)\,[e_{4}^{\textup{as}},e_{3}^{\textup{as}}]+\not{g}_{a,M}^{BC}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{B}(\epsilon)\,[e_{4}^{\textup{as}},e_{C}^{\textup{as}}]\,,

\displaystyle[\et(\epsilon),\ea(\epsilon)]=

\displaystyle\left(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}(\epsilon)(\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\textup{as}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon))+\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}(\epsilon)(\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{3}^{\textup{as}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon))+\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}^{B}(\epsilon)(\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{B}^{\textup{as}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon))\right.

\displaystyle+\left(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}(\epsilon)(\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\textup{as}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon))+\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}(\epsilon)(\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{3}^{\textup{as}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon))+\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}^{B}(\epsilon)(\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{B}^{\textup{as}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon))\right.

Proof.

\displaystyle+\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}^{B}(\epsilon)(\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{B}^{\text{as}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon))\,e_{4}^{\text{as}}+\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}^{B}(\epsilon)(\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{B}^{\text{as}}}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon))\,e_{3}^{\text{as}}+\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}^{B}(\epsilon)(\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{B}^{\text{as}}}\not{g}_{a,M}^{DC}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{D}(\epsilon))\,e_{C}^{\text{as}}

\displaystyle+(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}(\epsilon)\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\text{as}}}e_{4}^{\text{as}}+(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}(\epsilon)\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\text{as}}}e_{3}^{\text{as}}+\not{g}_{a,M}^{BC}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{B}(\epsilon)\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}(\epsilon)\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\text{as}}}e_{C}^{\text{as}}

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}(\epsilon)\,(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)\,\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{3}^{\text{as}}}e_{4}^{\text{as}}-\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}(\epsilon)\,(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)\,\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{3}^{\text{as}}}e_{3}^{\text{as}}-\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}^{B}(\epsilon)\,(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)\,\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{3}^{\text{as}}}e_{B}^{\text{as}}

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}(\epsilon)\,\not{g}_{a,M}^{BC}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{B}(\epsilon)\,\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{C}^{\text{as}}}e_{4}^{\text{as}}-\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}(\epsilon)\,\not{g}_{a,M}^{BC}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{B}(\epsilon)\,\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{C}^{\text{as}}}e_{3}^{\text{as}}-\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}^{D}(\epsilon)\,\not{g}_{a,M}^{BC}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})_{B}(\epsilon)\,\nabla^{a,M}_{e_{C}^{\text{as}}}e_{D}^{\text{as}}\,.

One concludes the proof by rearranging the terms.

∎

Proposition 7.54
.

We have the identities

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\not{M}{}_{A}^{B}}(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{g}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}(\epsilon)\,g_{a,M}^{DE}\,g_{a,M}([\ef(\epsilon),\ea(\epsilon)],e^{\textup{as}}_{D})\,\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\textup{as}}_{E},\boldsymbol{e_{C}}(\epsilon))

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,g_{a,M}([\ef(\epsilon),\ea(\epsilon)],e^{\textup{as}}_{4})\,\boldsymbol{g}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}(\epsilon)\,\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\textup{as}}_{3},\boldsymbol{e_{C}}(\epsilon))

\displaystyle+\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}(\epsilon)\,\boldsymbol{\chi}_{\boldsymbol{AC}}(\epsilon)\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\not{\underline{M}}{}_{A}^{B}}(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{g}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}(\epsilon)\,g_{a,M}^{DE}\,g_{a,M}([\et(\epsilon),\ea(\epsilon)],e^{\textup{as}}_{D})\,\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\textup{as}}_{E},\boldsymbol{e_{C}}(\epsilon))

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,g_{a,M}([\et(\epsilon),\ea(\epsilon)],e^{\textup{as}}_{4})\,\boldsymbol{g}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}(\epsilon)\,\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\textup{as}}_{3},\boldsymbol{e_{C}}(\epsilon))

\displaystyle+\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}(\epsilon)\,\boldsymbol{\chib}_{\boldsymbol{AC}}(\epsilon)\,.

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\not{M}{}_{A}^{B}}=

\displaystyle\,\not{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}\not{\boldsymbol{g}}(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\ea,\boldsymbol{e_{C}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{g}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}\boldsymbol{g}(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\ea,\boldsymbol{e_{C}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{g}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}\boldsymbol{g}([\ef,\ea],\boldsymbol{e_{C}})+\boldsymbol{g}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{\boldsymbol{AC}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{g}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}g_{a,M}^{DE}\,g_{a,M}([\ef,\ea],e^{\text{as}}_{D})\,\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\text{as}}_{E},\boldsymbol{e_{C}})-\frac{1}{2}\,g_{a,M}([\ef,\ea],e^{\text{as}}_{3})\,\boldsymbol{g}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\text{as}}_{4},\boldsymbol{e_{C}})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,g_{a,M}([\ef,\ea],e^{\text{as}}_{4})\,\boldsymbol{g}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}\boldsymbol{g}(e^{\text{as}}_{3},\boldsymbol{e_{C}})+\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{\boldsymbol{BC}}\boldsymbol{\chi}_{\boldsymbol{AC}}\,.

The identity for
\boldsymbol{\not{\underline{M}}{}_{A}^{B}}(\epsilon)
follows from an analogous computation.

∎

7.5

An isometry between horizontal tensor bundles

We summarise our geometric procedure as follows. For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}

k
-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
, our procedure yields the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}

k
-tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
such that

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(e_{A_{1}}^{\text{as}},\ldots,e_{A_{k}}^{\text{as}})=\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\boldsymbol{G}_{A_{1}},\ldots,\boldsymbol{G}_{A_{k}})

(271)

and

|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}|^{2}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}=|\boldsymbol{\varsigma}|^{2}_{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}\,,

(272)

where the identity (
271
) follows from Proposition
7.5
and the identity (
272
) from Proposition
7.47
(see, in particular, the identity (
247
)). For
\epsilon=0
, we have
\boldsymbol{G}_{A_{i}}(0)=e_{A_{i}}^{\text{as}}
from the identity (
215
), and thus

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(0)=\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(0)\,.

From a more abstract perspective, our geometric procedure provides, for each
\epsilon
, the bundle morphism

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}(\epsilon):\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}

\displaystyle(p,(e_{A}^{\text{as}})_{p})

\displaystyle\mapsto(p,(\boldsymbol{G}_{A}(\epsilon))_{p})

between horizontal distributions on
\mathcal{M}^{*}
and the bundle morphism

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{k}(\epsilon):(\otimes_{k}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)})^{\star},|\cdot|_{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}(\epsilon)})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\otimes_{k}(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}})^{\star},|\cdot|_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)})

\displaystyle(p,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}_{p}(\epsilon))

\displaystyle\mapsto(p,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}_{p}(\epsilon))

between normed horizontal tensor bundles on
\mathcal{M}^{*}
. In fact, the bundle morphism
\mathfrak{B}_{k}(\epsilon)
is an
isometry
. For
\epsilon=0
, the bundle morphism
\mathfrak{B}_{k}(0)
coincides with the identity map over
\otimes_{k}(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}})^{\star}
.

8

The renormalised vacuum Einstein equations

Consider the fixed ambient manifold
\mathcal{M}^{*}
and the one-parameter family of metrics
\boldsymbol{g}(\epsilon)
and associated frames
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)
on
\mathcal{M}^{*}
introduced in Section
6
. We assume that the metrics
\boldsymbol{g}(\epsilon)
solve the vacuum Einstein equations

\boldsymbol{\ric}(\boldsymbol{g}(\epsilon))=0

for all
\epsilon
. The nonlinear system of null structure and Bianchi equations for the connection coefficients and curvature components of the metrics
\boldsymbol{g}(\epsilon)
relative to the frames
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)
is a system of equations for smooth scalar functions and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
covariant tensors and coincides, for each
\epsilon
, with the system of equations presented in Section
4.7
.

Remark 8.1
.

Since
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)
is a family of
non-integrable
frames (see Section
6.3
), the formulation of the nonlinear system of null structure and Bianchi equations for the metrics
\boldsymbol{g}(\epsilon)
relative to the frames
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)
employs (and ultimately motivates) the geometric formalism from Section
4

in its full generality
.

We now want to
renormalise
the nonlinear system of equations relative to the Kerr background quantities, maintaining the equations in
geometric
form. To this end, the formalism developed in Section
7
becomes necessary. Indeed, when dealing with equations for covariant tensors, one needs to take differences of
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
covariant tensors and
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensors for
|\epsilon|>0
.

We apply the geometric procedure of Section
7
to each of the equations for
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
covariant tensors in the system, transforming each of the terms in these equations into a
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensor. Proposition
7.47
is essential to treat the various terms and give to the final
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensors a convenient form. The new nonlinear system of null structure and Bianchi equations for
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensors that one obtains is dubbed
reduced
system of equations and is presented in Section
8.1
.

Remark 8.2
.

In view of Proposition
7.47
, the reduced system of equations differs from the system that one would obtain by simply replacing the
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
covariant tensors (and differential operators) in the original system with their corresponding restricted (i.e. tilded) versions. The formulae from Proposition
7.47
account for the
extra terms
appearing in the reduced system of equations.

With the reduced system of equations at hand, one can derive the nonlinear system of equations in
renormalised
form. In the case of covariant tensors, one can indeed directly, and
geometrically
, subtract the equations for the Kerr background quantities to the corresponding reduced equations, all the equations being equations for
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensors (see Section
8.2
). The derivation of the renormalised system of equations is a straightforward (but lengthy) computation left to the reader. Nonetheless, we will comment on some remarkable structures of the system, which can be already deduced from the reduced equations of Section
8.1
.

8.1

The reduced system of equations

In this section, we present the
reduced
system of null structure and Bianchi equations.

8.1.1

Preliminary remarks

Some brief remarks before we present the equations:

•

We adopt the notation
\equiv
to denote an equality up to nonlinear terms. Among the nonlinear terms appearing in the equations, we only drop those arising from applying the identities of Proposition
7.47
and referred to as
nonlinear terms
in the identities of Proposition
7.47
.

•

In view of Remark
7.48
, the dropped nonlinear terms in the equations contain derivatives of the restricted
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
when arising from applying the identities of Proposition
7.47
to the restricted
angular
covariant derivative
(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)
. On the other hand, the dropped nonlinear terms arising from applying the identities of Proposition
7.47
to the restriction of the covariant derivatives
(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl_{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)
and
(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl_{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)
and Lie derivatives
(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}_{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)
and
(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}_{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)
do
not
contain derivatives of the restricted
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
. Thus,
for the reduced
null structure equations
with the form of
transport equations
, the dropped nonlinear terms containing derivatives of (restricted) connection coefficients are
only
generated by the restriction of the angular covariant derivatives on the right hand side
. In particular, for the reduced null structure equations with the form of transport equations in the
outgoing
direction, the dropped nonlinear terms containing derivatives of (restricted) connection coefficients
only
contain derivatives of
\boldsymbol{\omegah}(\epsilon)
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\etab}}(\epsilon)
.

•

In writing the reduced system of equations, we box the
extra terms
77
77

77

The
extra terms
are understood in the sense of Remark
8.2
.
generated by the identities of Proposition
7.47
. For example, we write the formula (
255
) as

\displaystyle-2\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\equiv

\displaystyle\,-2\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}

(273)

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}-{\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}-{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,.

Note that some of the boxed terms may be nonlinear when specialised to particular connection coefficients or curvature components, but will
not
be dropped.

•

When appearing on the right hand side of the reduced null structure equations with the form of transport equations in the
outgoing
direction, the boxed terms containing derivatives of connection coefficients will be re-written in a more convenient form. For example, the identity (
273
) will be, in such instances, re-written as

\displaystyle-2\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\equiv

\displaystyle\,-2\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}

(274)

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}-(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}+(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{\not{g}}\cdot\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}},\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}^{\boldsymbol{A}})_{\not{g}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}-(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\etab}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}},\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}^{\boldsymbol{A}})_{\not{g}}\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,,

where we used Propositions
7.47
and
7.49
. Relative to the identity (
273
), the identity (
274
) is more convenient because the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
replaces the
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
. This allows to apply the identities (
199
), (
200
) and (
201
) for the connection coefficients and conclude that the reduced null structure equations with the form of transport equations in the
outgoing
direction contain
no
derivatives of (restricted) connection coefficients on the right hand side other than derivatives of the
background
connection coefficients
\omegah_{a,M}
and
\etab_{a,M}
. This fact remains true when one takes into account the dropped nonlinear terms.

•

The identities (
199
), (
200
) and (
201
) for the connection coefficients are repeatedly applied throughout the reduced system of equations and allow for numerous simplifications.

•

Each of the quantities in the reduced system of equations depends on
\epsilon
. To keep the notation lighter, the
\epsilon
will be dropped throughout.

•

For future convenience, the reduced null structure equations corresponding to the equations (
95
), (
96
), (
98
) and (
99
) are presented in two alternative forms. See the related Remark
4.52
.

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}+\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chibh}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\etab_{a,M})+\frac{1}{2}(\textbf{tr}\boldsymbol{\chib})(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\etab_{a,M})-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})(\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\etab}})+2\,\omegah_{a,M}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}}

(291)

\displaystyle\boxed{+(\etab_{a,M},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{-(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}},\etab_{a,M})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{-(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\etab_{a,M})_{\not{g}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\etab_{a,M}-(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})(\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\etab}})=

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}+\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chibh}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}+\frac{1}{2}(\textbf{tr}\boldsymbol{\chib})\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})(\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\eta}})+2\,\omegah_{a,M}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}}

(292)

\displaystyle\boxed{+(\boldsymbol{\eta}-\boldsymbol{\etab})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\boldsymbol{\etab}_{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,\not{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\boldsymbol{g}(e_{3}^{\text{as}},\ea)\,\boldsymbol{\etab}_{\boldsymbol{B}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}(e_{4}^{\text{as}})_{\flat}}}\,\boxed{-(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}},\etab_{a,M})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{4}\omegabh}+\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{3}}\omegah_{a,M}\equiv 2(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\etab_{a,M})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}-2\,\omegah_{a,M}\boldsymbol{\omegabh}-2(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\etab_{a,M},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}-2\boldsymbol{\rho}

(293)

and the transport equations for the torsion

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\equiv

\displaystyle\,-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\omegah_{a,M}+\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chih}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,(\etab_{a,M}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})+\frac{1}{2}(\textbf{tr}\boldsymbol{\chi})(\etab_{a,M}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})(\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\etab}}-\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\zeta}})-\omegah_{a,M}(\etab_{a,M}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}

(294)

\displaystyle\boxed{+(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}},\etab_{a,M})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\omegah_{a,M})\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\omegah_{a,M})\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\equiv

\displaystyle\,-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\omegah_{a,M}+\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chih}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,\etab_{a,M}+\frac{1}{2}(\textbf{tr}\boldsymbol{\chi})\,\etab_{a,M}+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})(\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\etab}})-\omegah_{a,M}(\etab_{a,M}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}

(295)

\displaystyle\boxed{+(\boldsymbol{\etab}-\boldsymbol{\eta})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,\not{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\boldsymbol{g}(e_{3}^{\text{as}},\ea)\,\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\boldsymbol{B}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}(e_{4}^{\text{as}})_{\flat}}}\,\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\omegah_{a,M})\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\omegah_{a,M})\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\equiv

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\boldsymbol{\omegabh}+\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chih}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}+\frac{1}{2}(\textbf{tr}\boldsymbol{\chi})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})(\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\yb}})-\omegah_{a,M}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}+\boldsymbol{\omegabh}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}}

(296)

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chibh}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})-\frac{1}{2}(\textbf{tr}\boldsymbol{\chib})(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})(\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\eta}}+\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\zeta}})

\displaystyle\boxed{+(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{-(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})_{\not{g}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\omegabh})\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\omegabh})\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\equiv

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\boldsymbol{\omegabh}+\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chih}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}+\frac{1}{2}(\textbf{tr}\boldsymbol{\chi})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})(\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\yb}})-\omegah_{a,M}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}+\boldsymbol{\omegabh}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}}

(297)

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chibh}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\frac{1}{2}(\textbf{tr}\boldsymbol{\chib})\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})\,(\widetilde{{}^{\star}\boldsymbol{\eta}})

\displaystyle\boxed{+(\boldsymbol{\eta}-\boldsymbol{\etab})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,\not{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\boldsymbol{g}(e_{3}^{\text{as}},\ea)\,\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{\boldsymbol{B}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}(e_{4}^{\text{as}})_{\flat}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\omegabh})\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\omegabh})\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

and the elliptic equation for the torsion

\displaystyle\widetilde{\not{\textbf{curl}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\equiv

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{2}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chih}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chibh}}+\frac{1}{4}\,(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chi})(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})-\frac{1}{4}\,(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chib})(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})\,\omegah_{a,M}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})\,\boldsymbol{\omegabh}+\boldsymbol{\sigma}

(298)

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\zeta}-{\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\zeta})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\zeta}-{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\zeta})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})_{\not{g}}\,{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{B}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,.

We have the Codazzi equations

\displaystyle\widetilde{\not{\textbf{div}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chih}}\equiv

\displaystyle\,-\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chih}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\zeta}}-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{{}^{\star}\zeta}})+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chi})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}-(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\eta}})-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}

(299)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chi})\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{4}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chi}))\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{4}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chi}))\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}
