\displaystyle\widetilde{\not{\textbf{div}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chibh}}\equiv

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chibh}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\zeta}}+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\zeta}})-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chib})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}-(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\yb}})-(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\etab}})+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}}

(300)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chib})\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{4}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chib}))\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{4}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chib}))\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\varepsilon}}{}^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib}))\,\boxed{-\frac{1}{4}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib}))\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{-\frac{1}{4}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib}))\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

and the Gauss equation

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{K}}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chih}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chibh}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}-\frac{1}{4}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chi})(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chib})-\frac{1}{4}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})-\boldsymbol{\rho}\,.

(301)

8.1.3

Reduced Bianchi equations

The reduced Bianchi equations read

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{3}\widetilde{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chib})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}+2\boldsymbol{\omegabh\widetilde{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})(\widetilde{{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\alpha}}})\equiv

\displaystyle\,-2\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}-3\,\boldsymbol{\rho}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chih}}-3\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\widetilde{{{}\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\chih}}})+(4\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})\boldsymbol{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}

(302)

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\beta}-{\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\beta}-{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{4}\widetilde{\beta}}+2(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chi})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\omegah_{a,M}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-2(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\beta}})\equiv

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\textbf{div}}}\,\widetilde{\alpha}}+(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\etab}{}^{\sharp}}+2\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\zeta}{}^{\sharp}})\boldsymbol{\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\alpha}}\,\boxed{+(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\etab_{a,M})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,,

(303)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{3}\widetilde{\beta}}+(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chib})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\boldsymbol{\omegabh}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\beta}})\equiv

\displaystyle\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\boldsymbol{\rho}+\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\varepsilon}}{}^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\boldsymbol{\sigma})+3\,\boldsymbol{\rho}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}+3\,\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\eta}})+2\,\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chih}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\betab}}+\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\yb}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\widetilde{\alpha}}

(304)

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\boldsymbol{\rho})\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\boldsymbol{\rho})\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\boldsymbol{\sigma})\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\boldsymbol{\sigma})\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{+(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{-(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})_{\not{g}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{4}\rho}+\frac{3}{2}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chi})\boldsymbol{\rho}\equiv

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\textbf{div}}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}+(2\etab_{a,M}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}-\frac{1}{2}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chibh}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\alpha}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}-\frac{3}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})\boldsymbol{\sigma}

(305)

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\beta}-{\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,(\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}},\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\beta}-{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\boldsymbol{A}}(\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}},\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})_{\not{g}}\,{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}(\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}},\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{B}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{4}\sigma}+\frac{3}{2}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chi})\boldsymbol{\sigma}\equiv

\displaystyle\,-\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\textbf{curl}}}\,\widetilde{\beta}}-(2\etab_{a,M}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chibh}}\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}+\frac{3}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})\boldsymbol{\rho}

(306)

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\beta}-{\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\beta}-{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})_{\not{g}}\,{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{B}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{3}\rho}+\frac{3}{2}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chib})\boldsymbol{\rho}\equiv

\displaystyle\,-\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\textbf{div}}}\,\widetilde{\betab}}-(2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}-\frac{1}{2}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chih}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\alphab}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}+2(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}+\frac{3}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})\boldsymbol{\sigma}

(307)

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\betab}-{\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\betab})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,(\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}},\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\betab}-{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\betab})^{\boldsymbol{A}}(\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}},\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}})_{\not{g}}\,{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}(\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}},\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{B}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{3}\sigma}+\frac{3}{2}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chib})\boldsymbol{\sigma}\equiv

\displaystyle\,-\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\textbf{curl}}}\,\widetilde{\betab}}-(2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}}-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chih}}\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\alphab}}-2\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\frac{3}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})\boldsymbol{\rho}

(308)

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\betab}-{\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\betab})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\betab}-{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\betab})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}})_{\not{g}}\,{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{AB}}\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{A}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{B}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{4}\widetilde{\betab}}+(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chi})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}}+\omegah_{a,M}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}}+(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\betab}})\equiv

\displaystyle\,-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\boldsymbol{\rho}+\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\varepsilon}}{}^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\boldsymbol{\sigma})-3\boldsymbol{\rho}\etab_{a,M}+3\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\etab}})+2\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\chibh}{}^{\sharp}\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\widetilde{\beta}}

(309)

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\boldsymbol{\rho})\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\boldsymbol{\rho})\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\boldsymbol{\sigma})\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{+\frac{1}{2}\,(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\boldsymbol{\sigma})\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{+(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}},\etab_{a,M})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{3}\widetilde{\betab}}+2(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chib})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}}-\boldsymbol{\omegabh\widetilde{\betab}}-2(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\betab}})\equiv

\displaystyle\,-\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\textbf{div}}}\,\widetilde{\alphab}}-(\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\eta}{}^{\sharp}}-2\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\zeta}{}^{\sharp}})\boldsymbol{\cdot_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\alphab}}-3\boldsymbol{\rho}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\yb}}+3\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\yb}})

(310)

\displaystyle\boxed{+(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\cdot\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,\boxed{-(\boldsymbol{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}})_{\not{g}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\nablasl}_{4}\widetilde{\alphab}}+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\textbf{tr}\chi})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\alphab}}+2\omegah_{a,M}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\alphab}}-\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{}^{\star}\alphab}})\equiv

\displaystyle\,2\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\betab}}-3\,\boldsymbol{\rho}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\chibh}}+3\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{{{}^{\star}\chibh}}})-(4\etab_{a,M}-\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\zeta}})\boldsymbol{\widehat{\otimes}}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\betab}}

(311)

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\boldsymbol{\betab}-{\boldsymbol{\chib}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\betab})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\ef)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}

\displaystyle\boxed{-\frac{1}{2}\,(\boldsymbol{\nablasl}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\boldsymbol{\betab}-{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\boldsymbol{\betab})^{\boldsymbol{A}}\,\widetilde{(\et)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{(\ea)_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}}\,.

8.2

The system for the renormalised quantities

Using the reduced nonlinear system of equations of Section
8.1
, one can derive the
renormalised
system of equations.

For any smooth scalar function
\boldsymbol{f}(\epsilon)
and
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
in the reduced system of equations, we define the
renormalised quantities

\displaystyle\delta\boldsymbol{f}(\epsilon):

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{f}(\epsilon)-\boldsymbol{f}(0)\,,

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon):

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)-\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(0)\,.

(312)

We emphasise that
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
is a difference of
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensors for all
\epsilon
, and thus a
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensor for all
\epsilon
. In view of Remark
7.36
, we have

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(0)=0\,.

Applying the definition (
312
) to the induced metric
\boldsymbol{\not{g}}(\epsilon)
yields the renormalised quantity

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)-\not{g}_{a,M}\,.

Similarly, the definition (
312
) can be applied to any connection coefficient and curvature component in the reduced system of equations to define the corresponding renormalised quantity. The connection coefficient
\boldsymbol{\eta}(\epsilon)
, for instance, yields the renormalised quantity

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(\epsilon)=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(\epsilon)-\eta_{a,M}

(313)

and the corrected Gauss curvature
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{K}}}(\epsilon)
yields the renormalised quantity

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{K}}}(\epsilon)=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{K}}}(\epsilon)-\widetilde{\not{K}}_{a,M}\,.

We also define the
renormalised connection

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\epsilon)
on
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
as the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}

(1,2)
-
tensor

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\epsilon):=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\epsilon)-\nablasl_{a,M}\,.

The renormalised connection
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\epsilon)
on
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
is a
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}

tensor
since it is the difference of two linear connections on
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
. Moreover, in view of Remark
7.44
, we have the identically vanishing
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensor

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(0)=0\,.

Remark 8.3
.

The fact that the difference between two connections is a tensor only relies on both the connections being
linear
. As already pointed out, the connection
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\epsilon)
on
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
has
torsion
for all
\epsilon
, but this does
not
prevent
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\epsilon)
from being a
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}

tensor
.

For future convenience, we define the
renormalised frame commutators

\delta([\boldsymbol{e_{I}},\boldsymbol{e_{J}}])(\epsilon):=[\boldsymbol{e_{I}}(\epsilon),\boldsymbol{e_{J}}(\epsilon)]-[e_{I}^{\text{as}},e_{J}^{\text{as}}]

for
\boldsymbol{I},\boldsymbol{J}=\left\{\boldsymbol{1},\boldsymbol{2},\boldsymbol{3},\boldsymbol{4}\right\}
.

Remark 8.4
.

All the renormalised quantities are
global
,
regular

\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\textup{as}}}
tensors on the Kerr exterior manifold
(\mathcal{M}^{*},g_{a,M})
.

The explicit derivation of the renormalised system of equations is omitted, but can be easily carried out by the reader. As for the reduced system of equations, the renormalised system of equations is a system of equations for
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensors.

Crucially, the renormalised null structure equations with the form of transport equations in the
outgoing
direction do
not
contain any derivatives of renormalised connection coefficients on the right hand side. This structure is already apparent from the reduced system of equations of Section
8.1
. In this regard, see the preliminary discussion in Section
8.1.1
.

A similar structure does
not
appear for the corresponding transport equations in the
ingoing
direction.

9

The linearisation procedure

In this section, we derive general linearisation formulae around
\epsilon=0
for the renormalised quantities defined in Section
8.2
. We already remark that, exploiting the geometric formulation of the renormalised quantities achieved in Section
7
, we will be able to linearise all renormalised quantities
geometrically
and write all linearisation formulae
in geometric form
. All the linearised quantities are
global
,
regular

\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensors on the Kerr exterior manifold
(\mathcal{M},g_{a,M})
.

In Section
10
, the formulae will be applied to linearise the renormalised vacuum Einstein equations around the Kerr exterior manifold
(\mathcal{M},g_{a,M})
relative to the frame
\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}
, i.e. around
\epsilon=0
.

9.1

Overview

The formal linearisation of the renormalised quantities is straightforward. However, some more work is needed if one wants to write certain linearised quantities in a convenient form. We comment on two such instances.

•

Let
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
be a
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensor. The linearisation of the renormalised covariant derivative

\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)

is the most technical part of the section. Crucially, we will be able to relate the linearisation of the renormalised connection
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\epsilon)
(which we recall is a
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensor) to the linearisation of the renormalised metric
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)
(see Proposition
9.11
). For this to be possible, the choice of induced connection
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}(\epsilon)
over
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
made in Section
7
plays a fundamental role. See the related preliminary comments in Section
7.1
.

•

Let
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
be a
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensor. We will
not
need general linearisation formulae for the renormalised covariant derivatives

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\ef}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)\,,

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}_{\et}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)\,.

(314)

In fact, when we later apply the linearisation formulae of the present section to linearise the renormalised vacuum Einstein equations, we
never
linearise the covariant derivatives (
314
)
unless

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(0)\equiv 0
. The renormalised versions of the equations (
95
), (
96
), (
98
) and (
99
) will be considered in their alternative form of Remark
4.52
, where one linearises the renormalised Lie derivatives

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\ef}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)\,,

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\ef}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)

(315)

instead (see Proposition
9.9
). The renormalised Lie derivatives (
315
) are more convenient to linearise than the renormalised covariant derivatives (
314
). This computational advantage is already manifest from the nonlinear formulae for
(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\et}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)
in Proposition
7.52
, where the differential operators on the right hand side are
independent of
\epsilon
.
78
78

78

The reader should realise that the dependence on
\epsilon
of the
Lie derivative
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{X}(\epsilon)}
only originates from the dependence on
\epsilon
of the vector field
\boldsymbol{X}(\epsilon)
relative to which the derivative is taken. In this regard, we recall that the Lie derivative
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{X}(\epsilon)}
arises from the spacetime Lie derivative
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{L}}_{\boldsymbol{X}(\epsilon)}
(whose dependence on
\epsilon
only originates from
\boldsymbol{X}(\epsilon)
) projected (and then restricted) over
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
, the projection being independent of
\epsilon
.
For
(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\ef}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)
, one has the very convenient identity

(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\ef}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)=(\not{\mathcal{L}}^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\text{as}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)\,.

To keep the notation lighter, all the quantities corresponding to
|\epsilon|=0
are simply denoted as de-bolded, de-tilded quantities. For instance, we write the
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensor
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(0)\equiv\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(0)
as
\varsigma
. We also often remove the reference to the Kerr parameters. For instance, we write the geometric quantities
\not{g}_{a,M}
,
\nablasl^{a,M}
and
\not{\mathcal{L}}^{a,M}
as
\not{g}
,
\nablasl
and
\not{\mathcal{L}}
respectively.

9.2

Scalar functions

We consider the scalar renormalised quantities
\delta\boldsymbol{f}(\epsilon)
from Section
8.2
. The one-parameter family
\delta\boldsymbol{f}(\epsilon)
is regarded as a smooth map in the
\epsilon
variable

\delta\boldsymbol{f}(\epsilon):\mathbb{R}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathcal{M})\,.

We consider the Taylor expansion of
\delta\boldsymbol{f}(\epsilon)
around
\epsilon=0
as follows

\delta\boldsymbol{f}(\epsilon)=\epsilon\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{f}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

The linearisation of
\delta\boldsymbol{f}(\epsilon)
consists in dropping the
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})
-terms in the expansion.

9.3

Covariant tensors

We consider the covariant renormalised quantities
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
from Section
8.2
. The one-parameter family
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
is regarded as a smooth map in the
\epsilon
variable

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon):\mathbb{R}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}})^{\star}\,.

(316)

We consider the Taylor expansion of
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
around
\epsilon=0
as follows

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)=\epsilon\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

The quantity
\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}
is a
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
one-tensor.
79
79

79

One has the identity
\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}=\partial_{\epsilon}\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)|_{\epsilon=0}
, with
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
a smooth function of
\epsilon
as in (
316
).
The linearisation of
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
consists in dropping the
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})
-terms in the expansion.

For the renormalised metrics
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)
, we can expand as follows

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}(\epsilon)=\epsilon\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

For the renormalised
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
one-tensors
\delta\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{I}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}(\epsilon)
, we can expand as follows

\delta\widetilde{(\boldsymbol{e_{I}})_{\boldsymbol{\flat}}}(\epsilon)=\epsilon\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{(e_{I})_{\flat}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})

for all
\boldsymbol{I},\boldsymbol{J}=\left\{\boldsymbol{1},\boldsymbol{2},\boldsymbol{3},\boldsymbol{4}\right\}
.

Remark 9.1
.

The linearisation of a
symmetric
covariant renormalised quantity
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
is a
symmetric

\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensor.

Remark 9.2
.

Let
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
be a symmetric
traceless
(relative to
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}(\epsilon)
)
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensor. The linearisation of the covariant renormalised quantity
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
is
not
, in general, a symmetric
traceless
(relative to
\not{g}^{-1}_{a,M}
)
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensor. However, if
\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)\equiv\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\epsilon)
(or, equivalently, if
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(0)\equiv 0
), then the linearised quantity
is
symmetric traceless. The linearised quantities

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\chih}\,,

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\chibh}\,,

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\alphab}

are
symmetric traceless
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensors, whereas the linearisations of the renormalised quantities

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})(\epsilon)\,,

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)

are
not
, in general, symmetric traceless
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensors (see the later general formulae (
335
) and (
349
)). The linearisation of special combinations of renormalised quantities, like for instance

\displaystyle\delta(-2\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}})(\epsilon)\,,

\displaystyle\delta(-2\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\etab}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\etab}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\etab}})(\epsilon)\,,

are
symmetric traceless
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensors (although the linearisation of the single terms separately are not). This latter fact follows from the identities

\displaystyle(-2\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}})(0)

\displaystyle\equiv 0\,,

\displaystyle(-2\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\etab}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\etab}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\etab}})(0)

\displaystyle\equiv 0

and can be checked via the general formula (
350
).

We define the smooth scalar function

\text{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}:=(\not{g}^{-1}){}^{AB}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}_{AB}

and the
symmetric traceless

\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
two-tensor

\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}:=\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}-\frac{1}{2}\,(\text{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\,\not{g}\,.

We remark that we always linearise fully covariant renormalised quantities, with the exception of the
renormalised inverse metric

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}(\epsilon):=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}(\epsilon)-\not{g}^{-1}_{a,M}\,,

whose Taylor expansion around
\epsilon=0
reads

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}^{-1}}(\epsilon)=\epsilon\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}^{-1}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

9.4

Frame coefficients

We Taylor-expand and linearise the renormalised quantities

\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}(\epsilon)\,,\,\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}(\epsilon)

as outlined in Section
9.2
.

The one-parameter families of renormalised
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
one-tensors
\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}(\epsilon)
and
\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)
are regarded as smooth maps in the
\epsilon
variable

\displaystyle\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}})^{*}\,,

\displaystyle\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}})^{*}\,.

(317)

We consider the Taylor expansion of
\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}(\epsilon)
and
\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)
around
\epsilon=0
as follows

\displaystyle\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{A}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

The quantities
\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}
and
\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{A}
are
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
one-tensors. The linearisation of
\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}(\epsilon)
and
\delta\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)
consists in dropping the
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})
-terms in the expansion.

We Taylor-expand and linearise the renormalised quantities

\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}}(\epsilon)\,,\,\delta\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}}(\epsilon)

as outlined in Section
9.3
.

9.5

Identities for linearised quantities

The following proposition is crucial.

Proposition 9.3
.

We have the identities for smooth scalar functions

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{f}}_{3}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{f}}_{4}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\omegah}

\displaystyle=0

(318)

and for
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
one-tensors

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{f}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\xi}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\etab}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(319)

Proof.

The identities (
197
), (
199
), (
200
) and (
201
) imply the identities

\displaystyle\delta\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{f}_{3}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\delta\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{f}_{4}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\delta\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{f}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\delta\,\boldsymbol{\omegah}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\delta\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\xi}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\delta\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\etab}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=0

for the respective renormalised quantities. The identities for the linearised quantities from the proposition follow immediately.

∎

The two following propositions provide useful relations between linearised quantities. The identities stated are repeatedly used in the remaining part of the section.

Proposition 9.4
.

We have the identities

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{(e_{4})_{\flat}}-2\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{4}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{(e_{3})_{\flat}}-2\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{3}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(320)

and

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\boldsymbol{g}(e_{3},e_{A})}-2\,(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{A}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{A}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}_{AB}+(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{A})_{B}+(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{B})_{A}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(321)

Proof.

We linearise the identities from Proposition
7.50
.

∎

Proposition 9.5
.

We have the identities

(\not{g}^{-1}_{a,M})^{BC}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}_{AC}=-(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}^{-1}})^{BC}(\not{g}_{a,M})_{AC}

(322)

and

\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{\varepsilon}}=\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\,\not{\varepsilon}_{a,M}\,.

(323)

In particular, we have the identity

\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}^{-1}}=-\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}{}^{\sharp\sharp}-\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\,\not{g}_{a,M}^{-1}\,.

(324)

Proof.

The first identity is immediate from linearising the identity (
245
). For the second identity, we linearise the identity from Proposition
7.51
and obtain

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{\varepsilon}}=

\displaystyle\,(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{1}+\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{1}^{\sharp}\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\otimes\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{2}+\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{1}\otimes(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{2}+\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{2}^{\sharp}\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})

\displaystyle-(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{2}+\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{2}^{\sharp}\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\otimes\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{1}-\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{2}\otimes(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{1}+\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{1}^{\sharp}\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,((\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{1})_{1}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}_{11}+(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{2})_{2}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}_{22})\,\not{\varepsilon}_{a,M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(-\frac{1}{2}\,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}_{11}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}_{11}-\frac{1}{2}\,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}_{22}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}_{22})\,\not{\varepsilon}_{a,M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\,(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}_{11}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}_{22})\,\not{\varepsilon}_{a,M}\,,

where in the penultimate equality we used the second of the identities (
321
). The last identity is obtained by observing that the identity (
322
) implies the identity

\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}^{-1}}=-\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}{}^{\sharp\sharp}

and by decomposing the linearised metric
\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}
on the right hand side into pure trace and symmetric traceless parts.

∎

9.6

Frame commutators

We have the following proposition for the linearisation of the renormalised frame commutators. We recall the definition of the renormalised frame commutators from Section
8.2
.

Proposition 9.6
.

We have the identities

\displaystyle\delta([\ef,\et])(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left((\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\text{as}}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})\,e_{4}^{\text{as}}+(\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\text{as}}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})\,e_{3}^{\text{as}}+\not{g}_{a,M}^{AB}(\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\text{as}}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{A})\,e_{B}^{\text{as}}\right.

(325)

\displaystyle\left.+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}\,[e_{4}^{\text{as}},e_{3}^{\text{as}}]+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}{}^{A}\,[e_{4}^{\text{as}},e_{A}^{\text{as}}]\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\delta([\ef,\ea])(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left((\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\text{as}}}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{A})\,e_{4}^{\text{as}}+(\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\text{as}}}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{A})\,e_{3}^{\text{as}}+\not{g}_{a,M}^{BC}(\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{4}^{\text{as}}}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{A})_{B})\,e_{C}^{\text{as}}\right.

(326)

\displaystyle\left.+(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{A}\,[e_{4}^{\text{as}},e_{3}^{\text{as}}]+\not{g}_{a,M}^{BC}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{A})_{B}\,[e_{4}^{\text{as}},e_{C}^{\text{as}}]\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\delta([\et,\ea])(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\left((\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{3}^{\text{as}}}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{A})-(\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{A}^{\text{as}}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})\right)e_{4}^{\text{as}}\right.

(327)

\displaystyle+\left((\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{3}^{\text{as}}}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{A})-(\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{A}^{\text{as}}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})\right)e_{3}^{\text{as}}

\displaystyle+\not{g}_{a,M}^{BC}\left((\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{3}^{\text{as}}}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{A})_{C})-(\nablasl^{a,M}_{e_{A}^{\text{as}}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{C})\right)e_{B}^{\text{as}}

\displaystyle+(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{A}\,[e_{3}^{\text{as}},e_{4}^{\text{as}}]+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}\,[e_{4}^{\text{as}},e_{A}^{\text{as}}]+\not{g}_{a,M}^{BC}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{B}\,[e_{C}^{\text{as}},e_{A}^{\text{as}}]

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}\,\delta_{A}^{C}+\not{g}_{a,M}^{BC}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{A})_{B}\right)\,[e_{3}^{\text{as}},e_{C}^{\text{as}}]\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

Proof.

We linearise the formulae of Proposition
7.53
. Before linearising, we use Proposition
7.49
to write

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=((\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})(\epsilon),(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}})(\epsilon))_{\not{g}_{a,M}}+\textup{nonlinear terms}\,,

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{\boldsymbol{A}}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=((\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{\boldsymbol{A}})(\epsilon),(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}})(\epsilon))_{\not{g}_{a,M}}+\textup{nonlinear terms}

in the formulae of Proposition
7.53
.

∎

By linearising the right hand side of the frame commutators (
73
), (
74
) and (
75
), one can easily prove the following proposition. The proof is an easy computation that employs Proposition
7.49
in the same way as it is used in the proof of Proposition
9.6
.

Proposition 9.7
.

We have the identities

\displaystyle\delta([\ef,\et])(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(-\left(2\,\not{g}_{a,M}^{AB}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{B},\eta-\etab)+2\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\eta}{}^{A}+2\,(\eta-\etab)^{C}\not{g}_{a,M}^{BA}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{C})_{B}+\omegah\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}{}^{A}\right)e_{A}^{\text{as}}\right.

(328)

\displaystyle-\left(\omegah\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}+2\,(\eta-\etab,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})\right)e_{3}^{\text{as}}

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\omegab}-\omegah\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}-2\,(\eta-\etab,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})\right)e_{4}^{\text{as}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\delta([\ef,\ea])(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\left(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{M}}{}_{A}^{B}-\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\chih}{}_{A}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\,(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\textup{tr}\chi})\,\delta_{A}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\,(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi})\,\not{\varepsilon}^{\sharp_{2}}{}_{A}^{B}+(\not{M}{}_{A}^{D}-\chi^{\sharp_{2}}{}_{A}^{D})\,\not{g}_{a,M}^{CB}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{D})_{C}\right)e_{B}^{\text{as}}\right.

(329)

\displaystyle+(\not{M}{}_{A}^{B}-\chi^{\sharp_{2}}{}_{A}^{B})(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{B}\,e_{3}^{\text{as}}

\displaystyle\left.+\left((\not{M}{}_{A}^{B}-\chi^{\sharp_{2}}{}_{A}^{B})(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{B}+(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}_{A},\etab+\zeta)+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\zeta}_{A}\right)e^{\text{as}}_{4}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\delta([\et,\ea])(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\left(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\underline{\not{M}}}{}_{A}^{B}-\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\chibh}{}_{A}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\,(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\textup{tr}\chib})\,\delta_{A}^{B}-\frac{1}{2}\,(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib})\,\not{\varepsilon}^{\sharp_{2}}{}_{A}^{B}+(\underline{\not{M}}{}_{A}^{D}-\chib^{\sharp_{2}}{}_{A}^{D})\not{g}_{a,M}^{CB}(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{D})_{C}\right)e_{B}^{\text{as}}\right.

(330)

\displaystyle+\left((\underline{\not{M}}{}_{A}^{B}-\chib^{\sharp_{2}}{}_{A}^{B})(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{B}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\eta}_{A}-\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\zeta}_{A}\right)e^{\text{as}}_{3}

\displaystyle\left.+\left((\underline{\not{M}}{}_{A}^{B}-\chib^{\sharp_{2}}{}_{A}^{B})(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{B}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\yb}_{A}\right)e_{4}^{\text{as}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

9.7

Contractions and products

We have the following proposition for the linearisation of the renormalised contractions and products of
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensors. The proof is a computation which, in particular, exploits Proposition
9.5
to rearrange several of the terms in the identities.

Proposition 9.8
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensors
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
and
\boldsymbol{\theta}(\epsilon)
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
two-tensor
\boldsymbol{\omega}(\epsilon)
, we have

\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})(\epsilon)=\epsilon\cdot(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}\otimes\theta+\varsigma\otimes\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\theta})+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})

(331)

and the identity between smooth scalar functions

\displaystyle\delta(\textbf{tr}\,{}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\omega}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\textup{tr}\,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\omega}-(\omega,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})-\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\,\omega)(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

(332)

\displaystyle\delta((\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left((\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma},\theta)+(\varsigma,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\theta})-\frac{1}{2}\,(\varsigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\theta,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})-\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\,(\varsigma,\theta)\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

(333)

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\wedge}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}\wedge\theta+\varsigma\wedge\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\theta}+\frac{1}{2}\,({}^{\star}\varsigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\theta,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})-\frac{1}{2}\,(\varsigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{}^{\star}\theta,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})-\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\,\varsigma\wedge\theta\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})

(334)

and the identity

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}\widehat{\otimes}\,\theta+\varsigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\theta}-(\varsigma,\theta)\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\varsigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\theta,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})\,\not{g}_{a,M}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

(335)

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
two-tensor
\boldsymbol{\theta}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}{}^{\boldsymbol{\sharp_{2}}}\boldsymbol{\cdot}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\cdot\left(\theta^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\theta}{}^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot\varsigma-(\theta\times\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot\varsigma-\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\,\theta^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot\varsigma\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

(336)

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{{}^{\boldsymbol{\star}}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\epsilon\cdot\left({}^{\star}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}-({}^{\star}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})^{\sharp_{1}}\cdot\varsigma\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

(337)

9.8

Covariant and Lie derivatives

We have the following propositions for the linearisation of the renormalised Lie derivatives and covariant derivatives of scalar functions and
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensors.

Proposition 9.9
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\not{\mathcal{L}}_{4}\,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

(338)

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\nablasl_{3}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}+\chib^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}+(\nablasl\varsigma)^{\sharp_{1}}\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}\right.

(339)

\displaystyle-(\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}})^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot\varsigma+\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\textup{div}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}})\,\varsigma+\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\textup{curl}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}})\,{}^{\star}\varsigma

\displaystyle\left.+(\nablasl_{4}\varsigma+\chi^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot\varsigma)\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}_{3}+(\nablasl_{3}\varsigma+\chib^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot\,\varsigma)\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}_{4}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

We have the identities

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\not{\mathcal{L}}_{4}\,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\mathcal{L}}_{4}\,(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}))\,\not{g}_{a,M}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\chi)(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\,\not{g}_{a,M}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

(340)

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{3}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\nablasl_{3}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\nablasl_{3}(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}))\,\not{g}_{a,M}\right.

(341)

\displaystyle+(\textup{tr}\chib)\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}+(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib)\,{}^{\star}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\chib)(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\,\not{g}_{a,M}

\displaystyle\left.-2\,(\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}})+(\not{\textup{div}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}})\,\not{g}_{a,M}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}_{3}(\textup{tr}\chi)\,\not{g}_{a,M}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}_{4}(\textup{tr}\chib)\,\not{g}_{a,M}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

Proof.

The proof is immediate by linearising the identities of Proposition
7.52
. Note that the differential operator
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}
does
not
depend on
\epsilon
.

∎

Remark 9.10
.

One can re-write the identities (
338
) and (
340
) as

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\nablasl_{4}\,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}+\chi^{\sharp_{2}}\cdot\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

(342)

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{L}}}}_{\boldsymbol{4}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\nablasl_{4}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\nablasl_{4}(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}))\,\not{g}_{a,M}\right.

(343)

\displaystyle\left.+(\textup{tr}\chi)\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}+(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi)\,{}^{\star}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\chi)(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\,\not{g}_{a,M}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

Proposition 9.11
.

For any smooth scalar function
\boldsymbol{f}(\epsilon)
, we have

\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\boldsymbol{f})(\epsilon)=\epsilon\cdot(\nablasl\,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{f})+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

(344)

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(-\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\textup{div}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma})\,\not{g}_{a,M}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\textup{curl}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma})\,\not{\varepsilon}_{a,M}\right.

(345)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\widehat{(\nablasl\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})^{\sharp_{3}}\cdot\varsigma}-\frac{1}{2}\,((\not{\textup{div}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})^{\sharp}\cdot\varsigma)\,\not{g}_{a,M}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\nablasl\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})^{\sharp_{1}}\cdot\varsigma-\frac{1}{4}\,\varsigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,(\nablasl(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}))

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\,(\textup{tr}\chi)\,(-2\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}},\varsigma)\,\not{g}_{a,M}-\frac{1}{4}\,(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi)\,(-2\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}},{}^{\star}\varsigma)\,\not{g}_{a,M}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\,(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi)\,({}^{\star}\varsigma)\,\widehat{\otimes}\,(-2\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}})+\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib)\,({}^{\star}\varsigma)\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\chib)(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4},\varsigma)\,\not{g}_{a,M}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib)(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4},{}^{\star}\varsigma)\,\not{g}_{a,M}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

Proof.

To prove the identity (
344
), it suffices to observe that the difference of two linear connections over
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
vanishes when applied to a scalar function. To prove the identity (
345
), we compute

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\nablasl}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{AB}

\displaystyle=(\boldsymbol{\nabla}(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ}))_{AB}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{A}((\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})_{B})-(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{A}e_{B})

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{A}((\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})_{B})-\not{g}_{a,M}^{CD}\,g_{a,M}(\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{A}e_{B},e_{C})\,(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})_{D}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\nabla}_{A}((\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})_{B})-\not{g}_{a,M}^{CD}\,g_{a,M}((\boldsymbol{\nabla}-\nabla_{a,M})_{A}e_{B},e_{C})\,(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})_{D}-\not{g}_{a,M}^{CD}\,g_{a,M}(\nabla_{A}e_{B},e_{C})\,(\Pi\boldsymbol{\varsigma}^{\circ})_{D}

\displaystyle=(\nablasl\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})_{AB}-\not{g}_{a,M}^{CD}\,g_{a,M}((\boldsymbol{\nabla}-\nabla_{a,M})_{A}e_{B},e_{C})\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}_{D}\,.

(346)

Note that the second term in (
346
) is tensorial in the indices
A
and
B
, as one expects for the difference of two linear connections. We have

\displaystyle g_{a,M}((\boldsymbol{\nabla}-\nabla_{a,M})_{A}e_{B},e_{C})=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}\,(\nablasl_{B}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})_{AC}+\frac{1}{2}\,(\nablasl_{A}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})_{BC}-\frac{1}{2}\,(\nablasl_{C}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})_{AB}\right.

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\,(\nablasl_{B}(\text{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}))(\not{g}_{a,M})_{AC}+\frac{1}{4}\,(\nablasl_{A}(\text{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}))(\not{g}_{a,M})_{BC}-\frac{1}{4}\,(\nablasl_{C}(\text{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}))(\not{g}_{a,M})_{AB}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\,(\text{tr}\chi)\,(-2(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{C}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{C})(\not{g}_{a,M})_{AB}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\,(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi)\,(-2(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{A}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{A})(\not{\varepsilon}_{a,M})_{BC}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\,(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi)\,(-2(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3})_{B}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{B})(\not{\varepsilon}_{a,M})_{AC}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,(\text{tr}\chib)(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{C}(\not{g}_{a,M})_{AB}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib)(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{A}(\not{\varepsilon}_{a,M})_{BC}

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib)\,(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4})_{B}(\not{\varepsilon}_{a,M})_{AC}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

The identity (
345
) follows by a simple rearrangement.

∎

Remark 9.12
.

Each of the terms in the formula (
345
) is either a symmetric traceless, pure trace (relative to
\not{g}_{a,M}^{-1}
) or antisymmetric
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
covariant tensor.

Proposition
9.11
can be used to compute the linearisation formulae for
all
the renormalised differential operators. In the following proposition, we collect the ones that will be necessary in the sequel.

Proposition 9.13
.

For any
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{D}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}(\epsilon)}
one-tensor
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\not{\textbf{div}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\not{\textup{div}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}+(\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}\,\varsigma,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})-(\not{\textup{div}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})^{\sharp}\cdot\varsigma-\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\textup{div}}\,\varsigma)(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})\right.

(347)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\chi)\,(-2\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}},\varsigma)-\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi)\,(-2\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}},{}^{\star}\varsigma)

\displaystyle\left.+(\textup{tr}\chib)(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4},\varsigma)+(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib)(\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4},{}^{\star}\varsigma)\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\delta(\widetilde{\not{\textbf{curl}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(\not{\textup{curl}}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}-\frac{1}{2}\,(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}})(\not{\textup{curl}}\,\varsigma)\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

(348)

\displaystyle-2\,\delta(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(-2\,(\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma})-(\not{\textup{div}}\,\varsigma)\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}-(\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}\,\varsigma,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})\,\not{g}_{a,M}\right.

(349)

\displaystyle-\widehat{(\nablasl\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})^{\sharp_{3}}\cdot\varsigma}+(\nablasl\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})^{\sharp_{1}}\cdot\varsigma-\frac{1}{2}\,\varsigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,(\nablasl(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}))

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi)\,({}^{\star}\varsigma)\,\widehat{\otimes}\,(-2\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}})+(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib)\,({}^{\star}\varsigma)\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,.

Remark 9.14
.

For future convenience, we report the identity

\displaystyle\delta(-2\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{\star}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}+\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\,\widehat{\otimes}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\not{g}}}}\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}})(\epsilon)=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon\cdot\left(-2\,(\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma})+2\,\varsigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\varsigma}\right.

(350)

\displaystyle-(\not{\textup{div}}\,\varsigma+(\varsigma,\varsigma))\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,(-2\,\not{\mathcal{D}}_{2}^{\star}\,\varsigma+\varsigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\varsigma,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})\,\not{g}_{a,M}

\displaystyle-\widehat{(\nablasl\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})^{\sharp_{3}}\cdot\varsigma}+(\nablasl\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\widehat{\not{g}}})^{\sharp_{1}}\cdot\varsigma-\frac{1}{2}\,\varsigma\,\widehat{\otimes}\,(\nablasl(\textup{tr}\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\not{g}}))

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{1}{2}\,(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chi)\,({}^{\star}\varsigma)\,\widehat{\otimes}\,(-2\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{3}+\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\underline{\mathfrak{f}}}})+(\not{\varepsilon}\cdot\chib)\,({}^{\star}\varsigma)\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\overset{\text{\scalebox{.6}{$(1)$}}}{\mathfrak{\not{\mathfrak{f}}}}_{4}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2})\,,

obtained by simply combining the identities (
335
) and (
349
).

10

The system of linearised gravity

With the linearisation formulae of Section
9
at hand, one can linearise the renormalised vacuum Einstein equations and derive the full system of linearised vacuum Einstein equations on the Kerr exterior manifold
(\mathcal{M}^{*},g_{a,M})
.

In this section, we present the full system of linearised gravity on
(\mathcal{M}^{*},g_{a,M})
. The system is written
in geometric form
. The unknowns of the system are
global
,
regular

\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{N}_{\text{as}}}
tensors on the Kerr exterior manifold
(\mathcal{M}^{*},g_{a,M})
. A complete list of the unknowns can be found in Table
2
.

A
solution
to the system of linearised gravity is a collection of all the linearised quantities in Table
2
.
